# Skills · P · part 53

866 entries, principle-product-design to prisma-review. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **principle-product-design**: UX and product design principles — Nielsen's 10 usability heuristics, visual hierarchy, interaction design patterns, design-system compliance, and responsive layout. Accessibility/WCAG conformance is delegated to `swe-workbench:principle-accessibility`. Auto-load when reviewing UX, evaluating usability, assessing visual hierarchy or information architecture, auditing design-system compliance, or planning user-facing features. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-product-design) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it**: Apply before any claim about code, systems, or history. No claim without same-turn evidence; a hedge is a trigger to verify, never a place to stop. Routes the four investigation products that build the evidence. Unlike the other principles this one auto-fires: it must intercept a claim as it forms, not be named after the fact. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-prove-it) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★8.2k · 2.6k installs · 91 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before declaring any task or fix done. A habit, not a generated gate: run the real artifact, read the actual value, inspect the diff. Never a self-report, proxy, or 'it compiles.' ([itsvedantkumar/vstack](https://github.com/itsvedantkumar/vstack/tree/HEAD/claude/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★5 · 3 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' Use this skill when the user asks for principle prove it works or the task clearly matches this workflow. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply when the actual candidate and observable result still need verification. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Verify completed work against the real artifact before declaring success. Use after implementation, configuration, migration, automation, or delegated work when compilation or self-report is not enough. ([3metaJun/mstack](https://github.com/3metaJun/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task and before declaring it done. Verify the real artifact by running the feature, reading the actual value, or inspecting the diff. Proxies, self-reports, and successful compilation do not prove the task works. ([hellsan631/skills](https://github.com/hellsan631/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Before you claim that a task is done or a change works, check the real artifact through its real entry point and give a VERIFIED, NOT VERIFIED, or INCONCLUSIVE verdict. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact in a real org (run the behavior, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, a self-report, or 'it compiles'. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before declaring an artifact complete or accepting a subagent's work. Claim an outcome only with evidence that directly supports it. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · 2 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Verify the requested behavior against the real artifact or surface whenever possible; a proxy check only proves the proxy. Use when verifying changes, completing tasks, or claiming something is fixed. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-push-not-poll**: Apply when designing, reviewing, or authoring any mechanism that waits on a long-running background action: a CI/build watcher, a deploy or merge poller, a status-check loop, an agent babysitting a backgrounded command. Prefer a push/wakeup signal over a tight poll loop. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-push-not-poll) · ★2)
- **principle-read-state-artifacts**: Apply when designing, reviewing, or authoring any mechanism that checks on work a background process already tracks: a babysit/watcher loop, a status check on a queue or worker, an agent verifying what a cron already did. Prefer reading the state artifact the worker already wrote (a ledger, digest, or status file) over re-deriving that state with live commands inside a session transcript. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-read-state-artifacts) · ★2)
- **principle-real-artifact**: Verify every claimed outcome against the real artifact — run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff — never against a proxy, a self-report, or a build pass. Apply after completing any task, before declaring done, and whenever reading evidence produced by a worker, a ticket, or a pipeline state. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-real-artifact) · ★4)
- **principle-record-effective-inputs**: Expose or retain every input that can change a result, and match evidence to deterministic, stochastic, concurrent, or externally controlled behavior. Mandatory when a durable, audited, replayed, reproduced, or cached result depends on time, randomness, versions, external outcomes, or execution order, or when a deterministic or stochastic contract is claimed. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-record-effective-inputs) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★8.2k · 2.4k installs · 23 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★497 · 71 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when a requirement breaks a foundational assumption and needs coherent redesign. ([jaydubya818/skillz](https://github.com/jaydubya818/skillz/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Rework the design under the assumption that the requirement existed on day one. ([hellsan631/skills](https://github.com/hellsan631/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: When you integrate a new requirement into an existing design, redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from the start instead of bolting it on. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Reshape the system as if the requirement had always been part of it. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · 2 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Design the shape you would choose if the requirement had existed from day one, then migrate toward that shape. Use when when integrating a new requirement into a design that keeps fighting it. ([TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone](https://github.com/TheOnlyFusionCube/potetos-for-everyone/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-refactoring**: Refactoring discipline — Fowler's catalog of behavior-preserving moves (Extract Function, Inline Variable, Move Function, Replace Conditional with Polymorphism, Introduce Parameter Object, Extract Class), the rule of three, characterization-tests-before-touching-legacy, and small-steps-with-green-between discipline. Distinct from principle-clean-code (naming/length aesthetics) and principle-design-patterns (GoF catalog selection). Auto-load when discussing refactoring strategy, code smells, characterization tests for legacy code, or behavior-preserving structural change. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-refactoring) · ★2)
- **principle-release-engineering**: Release engineering — semver discipline, rollout strategy, rollback planning, expand-contract for breaking changes, kill-switch, idempotent release automation, post-release verification. Auto-load when cutting a release, planning a breaking API change, writing release notes, choosing a version bump, using a feature flag as a kill-switch, or building release automation scripts. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-release-engineering) · ★2)
- **principle-report-the-disqualifier**: Apply when writing or gating any report that makes a causal claim or summarises a cumulative outcome — a backtest, an experiment write-up, a capacity or cost model, a postmortem, an eval summary, a benchmark table. The report must carry what would change the reader's decision: what observation would refute the claim, whether the path crossed a state it could not return from, what the headline was before and after the fix, and which unvaried assumption flips the sign. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-report-the-disqualifier) · ★2)
- **principle-resiliency**: Resiliency principles — fault tolerance, partial failure, failure domains, bulkheads, fail-fast vs fail-soft, health checks, liveness vs readiness probes, idempotency keys, safe retry, deduplication, exactly-once effects, token and leaky buckets, synchronized retries, and 429 responses. Auto-load when isolating dependencies with bulkheads, choosing fail-fast vs fail-soft, configuring health/readiness/liveness probes, making operations safely retryable with idempotency keys or dedup stores, preventing double execution, or adding jitter to prevent thundering-herd retry spikes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-resiliency) · ★2)
- **principle-respect-the-shared-runtime**: Apply to any Apex transaction design. Governor limits are uncatchable: a breach kills the transaction with System.LimitException and no try/catch recovers it. Design so limits are never approached; probe headroom with the Limits class. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-respect-the-shared-runtime))
- **principle-respect-transaction-boundaries**: Limit atomicity and isolation claims to resources in one enforced commit boundary. Mandatory when several changes or effects are claimed to commit or roll back together, a read-then-write invariant depends on isolation, or state and outbound intent are settled together. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-respect-transaction-boundaries) · ★1)
- **principle-scope-the-session**: Apply when a session's actual work drifts away from what it was opened to do — a side-investigation becomes the main thread, or an unrelated request gets absorbed into an existing session. Start a fresh session instead of letting scope creep silently expand the one you're in. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-scope-the-session) · ★2)
- **principle-security**: Security design principles — trust boundaries and input validation, secure defaults and defense in depth, lightweight threat modeling, cryptography hygiene, RBAC/ABAC authorization models, OAuth 2.0/OIDC token validation. Auto-load when designing auth, discussing authn or authz, handling secrets or credentials, defining trust boundaries, validating untrusted input, considering SSRF or CSRF, configuring TLS, weighing least-privilege trade-offs, choosing RBAC or ABAC, handling PII or GDPR data, or pinning deps and generating an SBOM. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-security) · ★2)
- **principle-selective-queries**: Apply when writing or reviewing SOQL or SOSL. Every query filters on indexed fields with positive operators, returns only the fields needed, and stays selective at production volume: no leading-wildcard LIKE, no negative operators, no formula fields in WHERE without a custom index. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-selective-queries))
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★8.2k · 2.3k installs · 89 copies)

Serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. - **principle-sequence-verifiable-units**: Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. - **principle-solid**: SOLID principles — SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP, DIP (single responsibility, open-closed, Liskov substitution, interface segregation, dependency inversion). Auto-load when designing classes, or discussing coupling, abstractions, or any SOLID violation. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-solid) · ★2)
- **principle-sql-preserve-query-cardinality**: Preserve root selection, child completeness, ordering and bounded query work when implementing or reviewing SQL aggregate reads, filters, joins or pagination. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-sql-preserve-query-cardinality) · ★1)
- **principle-sql-preserve-relational-integrity**: Model independently stored facts with enforceable SQL identities and relationships when designing or changing tables, constraints, migrations, or row mappings. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-sql-preserve-relational-integrity) · ★1)
- **principle-sql-respect-transaction-ownership**: Preserve the caller's atomic operation when implementing or reviewing SQL transaction composition, participating readers and writers, or rollback behavior. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-sql-respect-transaction-ownership) · ★1)
- **principle-sql-use-jsonb-deliberately**: Establish a versioned, queryable storage contract when introducing or changing meaningful JSONB payloads, their readers, migrations or external query projections. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-sql-use-jsonb-deliberately) · ★1)
- **principle-state-consistency-contracts**: State the consistency, freshness, ordering, authority, and fallback required by each read or decision. Mandatory when code reads replicas, caches, projections, asynchronously synchronized state, or any source that may lag or disagree. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-state-consistency-contracts) · ★1)
- **principle-structure-by-ownership**: Apply when placing code, shaping monorepo packages, or reorganizing feature folders and imports. Make ownership, sharing scope, and public boundaries visible in the structure. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-structure-by-ownership) · 2 copies)
- **principle-subagent-inherits-scope**: Apply when spawning or prompting a subagent, fork, explorer, investigator, judge, or worker. A subagent may not act outside the scope its parent gave it, and may not widen that scope on its own. A subagent that finds the parent's premise wrong surfaces the contradiction with evidence instead of acting on it or quietly conforming to it. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-subagent-inherits-scope) · ★2)
- **principle-subtract-before-you-add**: Apply when sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead code, redundant validators, and stub references first, then build on the simpler base. - **principle-tagging**: Tag design references by principle, not aesthetic. Build a library you can actually search. Use when saving references, organizing inspiration, or building a design library that returns useful results. ([Dragoon0x/taste-skills](https://github.com/Dragoon0x/taste-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/reference-building/principle-tagging) · ★4)
- **principle-tdd**: Test-Driven Development (TDD) — red-green-refactor, spec first, Arrange-Act-Assert; making tests fast and isolated; test doubles (mock, stub, fake, spy), mocking-as-design-feedback, outside-in vs inside-out TDD. Auto-load when implementing a feature TDD-style, fixing a bug with tests, or writing the test before the implementation. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-tdd) · ★2)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Assert what the code's users can observe, so that the test fails when that behavior breaks and keeps passing when only the internals change. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation) · ★8.2k · 19 copies)
- **principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**: Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Identify a relevant defect and check that the test detects it.
